# Admiral Log — `epic-298`

Contract: `.agent-work/epic-298/LATITUDE_CONTRACT.md` · Plan: `<wave plan — set at latitude>`

The run's audit trail and the lessons audit's primary input. Append entries **as they
happen** — an unlogged ruling didn't happen. Own errors in the open: an ADMIRAL ERROR
entry that names the mistake and the fix is a closeout asset, not a liability.

Entry grammar (one line of date + tag, then the substance):

- `RULING` — an adjudication inside delegated latitude: what was decided, under which decision class, and why.
- `WAVE` — a wave launched: commanders, issues, worktrees, key launch-order terms (pre-rulings, fences, budgets).
- `INCIDENT` — a commander/crew death, stall, collision, or environmental kill: what died, autopsy, recovery action.
- `MERGE` — a PR merged: checks gated on exit code, diff verified in-fence, merge style and why.
- `ADMIRAL ERROR` — a mistake you own: what happened, cost, immediate fix, lesson candidate.
- `CHECKPOINT` — a contract checkpoint reached: what was presented, what the human decided.
- `ESCALATION` — a surfaced or out-of-taxonomy decision sent to the human, and the answer.

- 2026-07-31 — `RULING`: **`durable_root()` cross-worktree durability is a hard requirement**, and #301's acceptance exercise must cross a **worktree** boundary, not merely a process boundary. Class: delegated — this is a correctness constraint on the mission, not a design convergence, so it is mine to rule and I ruled it rather than adding it to Tommy's queue. Grounding is live in this wave: three commanders in three worktrees, and under an active Admiral epic lease `durable_root()` deliberately returns the **worktree** root (main checkout fenced read-only), which is exactly the condition producing N siloed stores. 301's underlying catch is the real defect — **every candidate's "cross-session" exercise tested only a new process in the same working directory**, so a store could pass green while siloing per worktree. Flagged to 301 the genuine tension this creates (one findable store vs. a read-only main checkout during the lease) with instruction to return it as a finding rather than weaken the exercise to fit.
- 2026-07-31 — `ESCALATION` (supersedes the earlier one): to Tommy — **revised** #301 convergence recommendation: D's record shape + **A's `active/`→`retired/` directory split** for retirement + 5 grafts (C writer-enforced partition, A always-present headings, mandatory validated retire reason, A single-line enforcement on agent-supplied free text, B per-field assertion addressability **for the agent-supplied bin only**) + `durable_root()` as an explicit requirement. 301's lean on the split sub-question **flipped from D to A** after it found its original reason was factually wrong (no candidate cross-references by path; all resolve ids at read time). New argument for A: "which set is this episode in" is a **filesystem** fact under A but a **content-parsing** fact under D, and every silent-failure mode found is a content-parsing failure — validation fails open the moment a file is hand-edited or a future write path skips the validator. Graft 4's concrete failure mode: D's negative filter means an observed-behavior field quoting a transcript containing the literal line `- status: retired` silently drops a fully active episode, no error. Graft 5's synthesis (the panel's best output): **the partition itself tells you which bin needs assertion machinery** — agent-supplied claims get disputed so they need individual standing; mechanical facts do not, so they stay flat key-value lines and pay none of the ceremony. That converts pre-ruling `decision:no-foreclosure-is-testable` from a box-tick into a constraint that actually shaped the design.

- 2026-07-31 — `ESCALATION`: **commander-300 design-it-twice float** (3-candidate panel: A minimal-interface, B ports-and-adapters, C common-caller-first; comparison at `298-300/.agent-work/300/DIT-COMPARISON.md`). Correctly did **not** self-converge. Five findings converged independently and verified against live bytes — treated as settled, not open: (1) **revision identity = git blob OID of LF-normalised bytes computed in-process**, `sha1(b"blob %d\0" % len(lf) + lf)`, verified equal to `git hash-object` under this repo's `* text=auto`/`core.autocrlf=true`; covers tracked, dirty, untracked, gitignored and out-of-repo files with no case analysis and no git subprocess, and **structurally eliminates CRLF** — the corpus's largest named irreproducibility source — rather than excluding it. A commit SHA was independently rejected by all three (lies about dirty trees; says nothing about untracked `docs/agents/` or gitignored `.agent-work/LESSONS.md`). (2) declaration is a new **optional** ordered list on the spine task; absent → empty manifest, so every existing spine keeps working. (3) **no globs ever** — a glob imports filesystem ordering, the second named irreproducibility source; declaration order **is content** and is never sorted (doctrine has reading precedence). (4) **the imperative prose is not deleted** — all three found the real `context` imperative carries rules a path list cannot express (the substitute-and-record rule when `docs/agents/` is absent; "a missing engine-config is a sanctioned degradation, do NOT create the overlay file"), so prose stays and a **mechanical lint** pins declaration against prose. (5) **metadata only, never file content** — the record says what was made available at which revision; it is not an archive and carries no claim of use. Recommendation is a named hybrid, **"C's two artifacts, A's row, B's resolver"**: committed content-only `skills/<role>/CONTEXT_PROJECTION.json` (zero varying fields, empty exclusion set) + run-local `.agent-work/<work-id>/context/<step>.json` whose single `/run` subtree is the entire exclusion set; A's minimal `{root, path, rev}` row (dropping C's `bytes`/`canon`/`state`); one pure producer selecting via the **existing** `active_id(cl)` — satisfying `decision:extend-dont-parallel` with no second selector — behind B's injectable resolver, with B's speculative Port A and two-encoder split dropped (B's own author disowned Port A: *"it currently buys nothing but symmetry"*).
- 2026-07-31 — `RULING`: recorded **the load-bearing amendment #300's comparison produced, which no single candidate had**: `rev` for a non-tracked file **varies by environment**, so it cannot live in the committed artifact. Evidence is concrete and from this very wave — `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR_CONTEXT.md` is *absent* in the worktree and *untracked-but-present* in the main checkout; candidate A recorded a real OID (`2a5ed203…`) and candidate C recorded `state: "absent"` for the same file. Both are honest about their own environment and **disagree with each other**, which would have false-FAILed the drift check (issue #306) on the next machine. Fix falls out of spec B2's own ahead-of-time-vs-per-run distinction: committed artifact resolves `rev` **only from the git object DB** (untracked → `null`, a pure function of the object DB); run manifest resolves from the **bytes actually delivered**. Class: this is a **finding**, not a convergence — the comparison's genuine yield, and precisely the value design-it-twice is supposed to produce.

- 2026-07-31 — `ADMIRAL ERROR`: **my `durable_root()` ruling was mechanism-wrong.** I ruled `durable_root()` reuse a hard requirement *to prevent* N siloed stores; commander-301 verified at HEAD that it **produces** that condition, and I then confirmed it independently rather than accepting the report: `scripts/agent_work_root.py` epic-lease branch — `if _active_epic_lease(main_checkout): return fallback` — whose own docstring says the exception exists so a write **gate** is not pointed at an unwritable path. Lesson candidate: the verify-against-code discipline binds the **Admiral's own rulings**, not just a Commander's reading of a launch order.

- 2026-07-31 — `RULING`: **spec Assumption 5 is weaker than the confirmed spec reads** — an epic-level finding from #300's grep-before-plan, carried to Tommy. The spec calls "the spine already knows which job is active, so it is the selector" *partially grounded* and leans on it to justify B2. What exists at `b69e6c8`: deterministic **selection** is real (`current` = `render_human(state(cl))`, a pure state-projection port, `checklist_engine.py:1336–1471`). **Assembly is absent** — every canonical Markdown file is named only *inside imperative prose* and opened by hand; no machine-readable declaration, no assembler, no record of what was loaded at what revision. Negative greps: `projection` across `scripts/ skills/ docs/ tests/` returns only the engine's internal state projection and unrelated Charter vocabulary; `manifest` across `scripts/` returns only the installer's `TEMPLATES_MANIFEST.json` and `file_issue_set.py`'s issue-set manifest — neither is a context manifest. So Assumption 5's grounding covers **selection only**, not assembly. #300 is explicitly **not** an honest-null (premise genuinely unbuilt) but is **narrower than the issue reads**: the deterministic selector already exists; only declaration, assembly and record are missing.

- 2026-07-31 — `RULING`: **#301 store moves to a git-TRACKED repo-root `episodes/` path. This SUPERSEDES the option-(a) ruling above** (one live ruling, not two for a later reader to reconcile), and 301's carve-out-vs-stage-and-harvest float is **withdrawn, not answered**. Discovery: `.agent-work/` is gitignored at `.gitignore:1` and `git ls-files .agent-work/` returns **zero** files — nothing under it is in git, LESSONS.md included — yet all four design candidates placed the store there. As designed the store would **not** have been Markdown in git and would have died with the worktree, violating `decision:markdown-in-git`, the one storage ruling that is human-given and settled. **Verified independently at source before ratifying** (not accepted on report): `.gitignore` line 1 is `.agent-work/`; `git ls-files .agent-work/` = 0; `git check-ignore -v episodes/` exits **1** (not ignored) while `.agent-work/x.md` exits **0** naming `.gitignore:1`. Class: delegated — file layout of the store, inside 301's latitude; the new repo-root directory goes to Tommy as a checkpoint FYI, not a gate. **Why it is right and not merely cheaper:** it satisfies `markdown-in-git` **by construction rather than interpretation** (an ignored directory would have complied with the words while violating what they protect), and cross-worktree sharing provided by **git itself** is a stronger mechanism than the one I ruled — so the epic-lease exception, the read-only fence, and my own mistaken `durable_root()` ruling all stop being load-bearing at once. #318 stands regardless: the defect is general, and the abandoned-lease half still bites anything else reaching for that accessor.
- 2026-07-31 — `RULING`: recorded 301's diagnosis of the panel's blind spot, in its own terms, as a lesson candidate: the panel **inherited the location from the prior art**, and **copying the neighbour's location copied the one property the new store must not have** — LESSONS.md is a deliberately transitory inbox ("where lessons pass through, not where they live") while the spec's whole point for episodes is that "the structured episode outlives its consolidation." Four candidates compared record shapes rigorously under four distinct constraints; **none checked whether the chosen directory was tracked.** Generalization worth graduating at closeout: **a design-it-twice panel varies what it is told to vary and inherits everything it is not.** Mechanically: g1-integrate's check became `git ls-files episodes | grep -q .` — the earlier check would have passed on the documentation alone — and g3's exercise became seed-and-commit in one worktree, retrieve from a second via the ordinary git path, which is a genuine cross-worktree demonstration and, unlike the fence-blocked version, actually runnable. Asked not to weaken that exercise, 301 strengthened it.

- 2026-07-31 — `ADMIRAL ERROR`: I dispatched both prep agents to **return text only, write no files**, so when they died their work died with them and left nothing to adjudicate — not even a partial. - 2026-07-31 — `ESCALATION`: **#302 / spec Assumption 6 → Tommy, and this one can force spec rework.** The inventory found a genuine **third-bin candidate**: *"Fail visibly rather than emit plausible wrong output; no hidden fallback"* (`global-everyone.md` §Universal posture). It is stated absolutely and a silent plausible-wrong-output is textbook catastrophic — but no validator can judge "plausible but wrong" versus "correct" without already knowing ground truth, at which point the check is trivial and the violation could not have occurred. Every imaginable mechanization catches a **subset**, leaving an unbounded semantic residual. A weaker second candidate (scoped-nulls correctness) is rated borderline by the analyst itself — recoverable, so arguably not catastrophic. Counterweight, and it is a good one: `verify_worktree_isolation.py` shows a catastrophic invariant that looks unmechanizable **from inside one component** is mechanizable **from another vantage** — the doctrine's own "no engine chokepoint to refuse at" is locally true of `checklist_engine.py` but false of the harness, where `spine_rail.py` already intercepts that exact boundary. **The pivot is one ruling**: if "no plausible wrong output" is genuinely catastrophic-class, Assumption 6 is **contradicted** and B0.3 needs a named third bin (the spec forbids smoothing it away); if Tommy rules it out of the bin, Assumption 6 is **supported** with a punch-list of ordinary unbuilt-mechanism gaps (rows 9–16).

- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **the #312 review is the strongest evidence this wave that adversarial review beats confirmatory review.** `reviewer-303` did not merely re-run the suite — it built **four mutated copies of the verifier** in scratchpad (repo and worktree never touched) and proved the test has teeth: disabling the confirmed-by check, the Disposition check, and the Status check each made the mutant exit **0** on the corresponding fixture, so each assertion is genuinely load-bearing. - 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **PR #320 NOT merged — CI is RED, and the commander's honest local green did not predict it.** Gated on the check exit code and verified at source, per the merge invariant. **39 failed, 1229 passed, 2 skipped**, all one root cause: `TypeError: Path.read_text() got an unexpected keyword argument 'newline'` at `scripts/query_episodes.py:120`. The `newline=` kwarg was added to `pathlib.Path.read_text()` in **Python 3.13**. Measured on this host: `python -V` → **3.14.3** (what the commander ran — its 1223-pass result was honest), `py -V` → **3.12.13**, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml:34` pins **3.12**. Returned to commander-301 to repair, with two carried constraints: the replacement must preserve the exact newline semantics keeping the g2 guard and the parser agreeing (a fix that changes them re-opens the U+2028 corruption already closed), and it must add a recurrence guard (CI matrix floor or an enforced minimum-Python assertion). g4 stays held on Tommy; the portability fix is independent of the retirement layout, so it costs no rework either way.
- 2026-08-01 — `ADMIRAL ERROR` / **finding bigger than the issue that surfaced it**: **issue #313's guidance is double-edged and I propagated it.** #313 (filed this wave) says `py -m pytest` false-reds and `python -m pytest` is green — and I relayed that to `reviewer-303` and into my own merge gating as if it were simply a correction. But `py` resolves to **3.12**, which is *exactly what CI pins*, while `python` is **3.14.3** — one to two minor versions **ahead** of CI. So the recommended invocation silently hides every portability defect of this class, and is precisely how #320's 39 failures reached CI unseen. The "false red" #313 describes and this "false green" are **the same underlying problem**: two interpreters that are not the same environment. The 24 corpus docs prescribing `py -m pytest` and #313's correction are **both wrong, in opposite directions**. Cost: none shipped (the merge gate caught it), but I had already carried the bad guidance into two dispatches. Asked commander-301 to comment the version measurements onto #313, since they change what the right fix is.

- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **two findings from the repair that outrank the bug**, both staged and both added to #313. (a) **A launcher name resolves differently depending on who invokes it**: on this host `py` is **3.12 from the shell and 3.14 from inside a pytest subprocess** — same name, same machine, two answers, because the subprocess inherits a different PATH; and this host's `py` is **not the real Windows launcher** but a shim that rejects the `-3.12` selector outright. This directly undermines the "just name the right interpreter" fix I proposed on #313, and will bite whoever implements it. (b) **A guard that skips silently reads as coverage**: its first version discovered the interpreter by name, found nothing, and **SKIPPED — passing while providing zero coverage**. Now it accepts a candidate only if it *reports the floor version when asked*, with an explicit override honoured. (c) Third, smaller but the same shape: repairing the writer **broke the write-phase atomicity test**, which patched `Path.write_text` with a comment claiming it worked "either way" — untrue the moment the writer moved off that method, so the patch stopped firing and the test went red for a reason unrelated to what it tested. Now patches the module's own write seam. *A test should track the implementation's seam, not a stdlib detail.*

- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **two generalizations banked that outlive their incidents.** (a) *"A traceback reports where execution **stopped**, not where the defect **lives**"* — they coincide for a logic bug and diverge sharply for an **environment** bug, because an unavailable API is unavailable at **every** use while only the first reached use raises. Therefore the unit of repair is every use, found by grep, not the line in the stack; the rule is **after an environment-shaped failure, search for the pattern before fixing the instance.** Evidenced by this wave's 13-vs-1 call sites. (b) It banked **my** synthesis, constellation-scoped: *"a check that cannot fail is indistinguishable from a check that passed"* — three agents, three mechanisms, one shape (301's floor guard discovering by name and skipping green; #300's two vacuous postconditions; the round-trip lesson's tests that only ever see clean artifacts). Correctly scoped constellation **because it recurred across three separate agents in one epic**, which is shared-machinery signal rather than a local quirk, with the general repair named: **require a check to demonstrate it ran against something that could have failed it**, mutation-testing (break it, watch it go red, restore) being the cheap universal form. Its bank-reason points at the real fix so a future audit does not merely re-confirm it: if vacuous-pass checks keep appearing, the fix belongs in the **crew handoff templates** (require every new guard to be mutation-verified), not in a lesson each run rediscovers.

- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **the panel-inheritance lesson is now one lesson with two shapes, renamed `a-panel-inherits-what-it-was-not-told-to-vary`, and its second shape is the sharpest finding of this epic.** Shape 1 is the neighbour the panel copies (301's case); shape 2 is the brief the panel is handed (300's case), stated with the attribution I asked for rather than the diplomatic version: *"The assumption did not come from prior art the panel chose to copy — it came from the person asking the question, so no amount of candidate-side care could have surfaced it. **A brief author cannot audit their own framing by reading the candidates, because the candidates ARE the echo.**"* The structural consequence, which is what makes this more than an observation: **if the failure were candidate diligence, a better panel fixes it; because it is framing, a better panel makes it WORSE** — more candidates means more echoes, and the convergence author reads the volume as corroboration. **So the mitigation cannot be candidate-side at all: for the brief-inheritance shape, the shared-assumption check must be run by someone who did not write the brief.** Kept deliberately as **one** lesson with two shapes rather than split, because splitting would let a future reader fix the cheap half (grep the paths) and believe the class closed. **This indicts me directly — I wrote both briefs both panels worked from**, which means I am structurally unable to audit them, and any convergence step this epic graduates must name an independent auditor or it ships broken.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **recorded 301's diagnosis of the wave's three read-the-file-instead-of-assuming catches, which is not the lesson I would have drawn.** Across `durable_root()` (mine), `.agent-work/` being gitignored (301's), and `.gitattributes` (301's again): **in every case the documentation was accurate and the inference from it was wrong.** `durable_root`'s docstring correctly described its intent; LESSONS.md correctly described itself as a transitory inbox; `.gitattributes` was never consulted at all while the proposed remedy assumed its absence. **Nobody wrote anything false.** The error each time was **treating a description as a substitute for the state** — a different failure from trusting bad docs, with a different fix: *you cannot fix it by improving the docs.* This lands squarely on the epic's own thesis — B2 projects canonical truth into an agent's active surface, and a projection is a description; B0.1 already insists every transformation between canon and the active surface be deterministic and attributable, but this says something further about what a reader may **infer** from one.

Had I folded #300's content in against the old id at harvest I would have either created a second op for a defect we had just agreed is one identity, or **overwritten the two-shape version with the one-shape version** — the exact fork the ruling existed to prevent. **Corrected resolution:** `a-panel-inherits-what-it-was-not-told-to-vary` is the surviving identity; #300's `panel-convergence-can-be-inheritance-not-evidence` lands as a **confirm** against it; **no additional fold is needed** because the content is already merged. - 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **#301's trio verified at 11 ops** (2 confirms, 9 adds, **3 constellation-scoped**), snapshot re-taken at that count — and verified using the **id-and-op-count protocol I adopted one entry ago**, which is the first test of whether a mitigation this run invented actually gets used by its own author. New op: `stale-description-has-two-shapes-and-only-one-yields-to-verification`, banking the distinction I drew and it kept apart deliberately. **Shape 1 (read-once)**: one reader, one fact, stale because never re-read — three instances, all with *accurate documentation and a wrong inference* — **fix is verification**. **Shape 2 (two-writers-one-fact)**: two agents, one fact, stale because the owner wrote to it **between the reader's read and the reader's use** — my fork resolution and my snapshot, both — **fix is NOT verification**, because re-reading cannot close a window the change lands inside; it needs a protocol carrying the version forward. Its bank-reason defends against the specific mis-graduation: **shape 1 is already well covered by existing verification lessons and is included only so the contrast is legible; an audit that graduates shape 1 alone and calls the class closed has done the exact thing the lesson exists to prevent.** Same structural reasoning that kept the panel lesson whole — the cheap half has the obvious mechanical mitigation, so it is the half that gets fixed alone. Scoped **constellation** because shape 2 is a property of the **machinery, not anyone's diligence**: it appears only where two agents share a fact neither can see the other write, which *is* the worktree-and-harvest topology this corpus runs on.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: recorded, in the commander's framing, why the near-miss is worth more than its cost: **near-misses are the cheapest data this system will ever get** about a defect class that has so far cost nothing. It is therefore written as *insurance against loss, never a source of truth, re-taken at use rather than trusted.* A mitigation that falls to the very class it mitigates is unusually good evidence about that class.

Worth recording that a mitigation this run invented was **found insufficient by the same run that invented it, on its second application** — and that 301 volunteered the failure of its own proposal rather than letting me discover it, which is the shape-2 disclosure discipline applied to the mitigation for shape 2.

A successor reading "7 ops" either notices the mismatch (costs a check) or trusts it and **harvests believing it has everything**. - 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **adopted 301's structural fix and wrote it into the STATE_NOTE as an authoring rule, not just a correction.** Its diagnosis: this is the same shape-2 staleness defect again, but note **where** it landed — not in the ADMIRAL_LOG, which is written as rulings happen, but in the artifact rewritten **only at detach boundaries**. *The two have different write cadences, so the one that gets read in a disaster is the one that goes stale fastest.* Therefore: **the state note carries POINTERS, never copies, for anything a commander keeps writing to** — a count or a hash goes stale the moment the owner edits; a path cannot. Rewrote the note accordingly (paths to both staged deltas with "read them, do not trust a count from here"), and stamped the rule into the file itself with this incident as its grounding, so the next author inherits the reasoning rather than just the format. Also folded the wave's settled closeout decisions and paid-off invariants into the note, so a successor does not re-derive them.

- 2026-08-01 — `INCIDENT` (**near-miss I nearly caused; no cost, and the lesson is the point**): after releasing both commanders I read stale lease heartbeats (301 at 14:21, 300 at 14:26, clock at 14:48) **plus** two unanswered direct probes — after both had replied promptly to every message all wave — and was one step from calling them dead and relaunching continuations into their worktrees. **That would have put a second agent into a worktree holding uncommitted work** (300: new `scripts/context_manifest.py`, `tests/fixtures/context_declarations.json`, modified `COMMANDER_SPINE.template.json`; 301: modified `docs/EPISODE_STORE.md`, `episodes/README.md`, `scripts/apply_episode_delta.py`) — the two-commanders-one-worktree collision doctrine names as **data loss, not friction**. **What actually stopped me: filesystem mtimes.** 10 files modified in 300's worktree in the last 10 minutes, 7 in 301's. Both were **alive and working hard the entire time**.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **the engine lease heartbeat is a poor liveness signal for a working commander, and this run now has evidence.** Heartbeats refresh only on **mutating engine verbs**, while the actual work of a gate is file edits and crew dispatch — so a commander deep inside `g1-implement` or `g4-implement` can be maximally productive and look maximally dead. Unanswered messages compound the illusion: a commander mid-task receives the message but finishes its current work first, so silence reads as death exactly when the agent is busiest. **The liveness test is filesystem activity in the worktree** (`find -newermt`), not the heartbeat and not message latency; the heartbeat answers "who owns this checklist", not "is this agent alive". This is a **third** state alongside the two the shared doctrine names (idle-with-complete-artifacts = *done*; silence-with-incomplete-artifacts = *stalled*): **silent, non-heartbeating, and actively working** — where the correct action is to **do nothing and keep polling**. Carrying to closeout as a lesson candidate: the existing idle-subagent adjudication rule reads liveness from the wrong instrument.

- 2026-08-01 — `ESCALATION` → Tommy asked *"maybe I'm missing the point of the packing slip. what's in it and what relies on it?"*, and answering it properly **surfaced a circularity that may dissolve issue #306.** The manifest has four consumers: (1) **#305 episode capture** — the "what was loaded" field distinguishes *the agent ignored the doctrine* from *the agent never got it*, which need opposite fixes; (2) **#307 map-first measurement** — what map material was available at which revision, paired with transcript ordering, and the exact discriminator for Tommy's own field report (*"it pointed to some random template"*); (3) **the reviewable diff** of what a doctrine edit changes, since context is assembled from several files and editing one does not show the assembled result; (4) **#306's drift check**. **(1) and (2) are per-run records and are fully served by Tommy's `.agent-work/` ruling.** But (3) and (4) are a **matched pair, and circular**: a projection generated fresh from canon every run **cannot be stale** — staleness becomes possible *only because* a copy was committed. So **#306's drift check exists to guard a hazard that only exists if you take (3)**. Net: the committed copy buys exactly one thing — seeing in a PR what agents will now see — and costs an issue's worth of machinery to keep that copy honest. Tommy's ruling is therefore more coherent than my recommendation was, and **#306 largely dissolves rather than needing a rescope**. Surfaced with a recommendation to drop it to whatever survives (likely only "the generator is deterministic", which #300 already tests). **Not acted on** — scope change is a surfaced class.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: recorded that this is the **second time this wave that answering a plain-language question from the human found a real defect in the plan** (the first: restating the decisions plainly exposed that I had never stated the questions). Both times the mechanism was the same — being forced to say *what relies on this* in terms that assume no context. Lesson candidate for closeout, and it is about the human interface rather than the machinery: **explaining a design to someone without the build context is a design review, not a reporting chore.**

- 2026-08-01 — `CHECKPOINT`: **Tommy resolved the `.agent-work/` clarification in an unexpected direction and added a doctrine-versioning requirement.** Verbatim: *"we should ungit ignore this projects agent work. it's my general preference that it is saved. but we should also give doctrine a version so there's a little traceability. every doctrine file should be in git, there will be a constellation version and a repo specific version. practically, it's just the repo rev number of the last doctrine edit that is sufficient for a versioning system (heck it could just be the current repo version in totality for ease). doubling the saved files in git seems like us doing too much work."* Four consequences: (1) **`.agent-work/` gets un-gitignored here** — it is his general preference that it is saved; (2) **the no-committed-artifact ruling is corroborated**, independently, on effort grounds — "doubling the saved files … too much work"; (3) **doctrine needs a version**, with **repo revision explicitly declared sufficient**, and simplicity named as the goal; (4) he independently reached the same conclusion I had surfaced — a committed copy is redundant — which is what makes **#306's drift check dissolve** rather than get rescoped.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **relayed to commander-300 with my reading rather than a dictate, because the identity function is its interface.** The two questions are distinct and the manifest can carry both **without doubling anything in git** — two fields in a record that already exists: **keep the per-file blob OID** (its three authors independently rejected a commit SHA and verified the OID against live bytes; a commit SHA **lies about a dirty tree**, which is the normal condition of a repo mid-development, so `HEAD` and the loaded bytes can disagree) and **add the repo revision** as the human-facing traceability stamp Tommy asked for. Flagged the honest limit: repo rev alone is not truthful on a dirty tree, so it wants a dirty marker or a stated limitation. Explicitly told 300 **not** to treat this as licence to reopen the panel or replan — if it costs more than one field and one doc line, stop and return, because a versioning stamp is not worth a replan. Also noted that its identity function covering tracked/dirty/untracked/gitignored/out-of-repo **with no case analysis** is why the un-ignore changes nothing in its code — a design property paying off against a change nobody anticipated.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: measured the un-ignore before doing it (scope is a surfaced class and 2,144 files is not a silent commit): **`.agent-work/` holds 2,144 files / 23 MB**, of which `archive/` is 5.4 MB and `epic-298/` is 2.0 MB; **no file over 1 MB**, and 16 binary-ish files (`.pyc`) that should be excluded rather than committed. Surfaced to Tommy with the number before acting — un-ignoring wholesale is a 2,000-file commit, and the `.pyc` files and per-run scratch are the part worth a narrower rule.

- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **#299 baseline task set chosen under delegation** — Tommy: *"just pick up your favorite issue. I dont know them off hand."* Verified all candidates OPEN and unassigned at source before choosing. **Picked: #710, #715, #698 as real tests, #704 as a deliberate NEGATIVE CONTROL.** Reasoning: #715 is the strongest single test because it is literally a "which boundary owns this" question between two *named* components, which is precisely what a map represents; #710 spans a module family so a component boundary should beat a repo-wide grep; #698 is included but **scored on seam-finding only**, discarding its packaging/hygiene sub-concerns as measurement noise. **#704 is the methodological centrepiece, not filler** — single file, function-level, findable by filename alone, so a map *should not* help; if map-first shows the same lift there as on the real tests, the instrument is measuring something other than map value and the task set needs re-cutting. - 2026-08-01 — `WAVE`: **#300's g1 reviewer returned APPROVE** (0 blockers / 0 major / 0 minor after one rework; survey re-driven through the engine, lease released last), and commander-300 has committed g1 work at `75ee317`. - 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **the g1 reviewer found a new member of the wave's recurring failure family, and it is subtler than the three before it.** It ran **six** mutants rather than accepting the implementer's one: 6/6 killed, control green. But **four of the six *satisfied* the test's `assertNotEqual`** — a garbage digest is trivially "not equal" to the oracle — and died only on the two explanatory **equality** assertions. So the assertion everyone assumed was load-bearing was **not what held the test up**. This is distinct from the earlier three instances (a guard that skipped silently, two postconditions that passed with nothing built, tests that only see clean artifacts): here the check **can** fail and **does** run — it simply **fails to discriminate**. Call it the non-discriminating assertion; `a-check-that-cannot-fail-is-indistinguishable-from-one-that-passed` currently does not cover it, and the audit should widen it rather than add a sibling. It also ran two integrity checks **neither the implementer nor the Commander had**: confirming `_raw_blob_oid` uses its own `hashlib` call and never references the module under test, so it **cannot collude** with the value it verifies (had it wrapped `cm.rev`, those four mutants would have survived); and that `git hash-object` is invariant under `core.autocrlf=true|false|input` in- and out-of-tree, so the oracle is not hostage to a CI runner's git config — a hypothesis it **formed and killed** rather than leaving as a worry.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: recorded the reviewer's colon-rejection adjudication as a model of measuring instead of reasoning: a colon filename **cannot be created on Windows at all** (`open("a:b.md")` → `FileNotFoundError`; NTFS parses it as a stream), so under the Windows-corpus constraint the "rejects a legitimate POSIX filename" cost is **unreachable in this corpus, not merely unlikely** — plus 0 of 2,680 filenames across the declarable roots carry one. It also **declined** its own earlier suggestion after pricing it: a content scan would traverse three roots, two outside the repo, varying per install and absent in CI, for a class with **zero** instances — re-pointed at #301 where the bytes are already in hand. A reviewer withdrawing its own recommendation on measurement is the same discipline the commanders showed retracting their own claims.

Cost: none — re-copied from the commander's original and redid it with explicit `encoding='utf-8'` and `newline='\n'`. Notable because this repo's own doctrine warns about exactly this Windows encoding hazard and I hit it anyway **while performing a harvest step whose entire purpose is not losing data**. - 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: recorded #301's own last two findings, both after its merge. **g4 bound cleanly and the design's bet paid**: binding Tommy's ruling changed adapter bodies and required **zero** changes to any g3 retrieval primitive — the stop condition written for that possibility never fired, which retroactively vindicates the five-named-seams structure. - 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **corrected the harvested `verdict-301.md`, and the reason it was wrong is structural rather than anyone's oversight.** It read *"driven to `archive`, which is deliberately held"* — true when harvested, false ninety seconds later when the commander completed archive on my go-ahead. Fixed in place with a dated annotation explaining the original text, plus a §9 note that the harvest is discharged. **The finding, in the commander's words and adopted as standing instruction:** you must harvest *before* the release to be safe against a death, and the agent's last state changes *after* the release — so **a harvested verdict is guaranteed stale by exactly the closing sequence, every time, for every commander.** Cheapest fix: the harvester **re-reads the verdict's status line after the release and before the sweep** — it is the only field that changes in that window. This is the fifth instance of the stale-description shape and the first that is **inherent to the doctrine's own required ordering** rather than incidental.

- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **sharpened the liveness finding — a commander's SPINE heartbeat can be hours stale while it works, because it is driving a CHILD checklist with its own lease.** commander-300's `spine.json` lease read 14:26 at a 17:01 clock — 2.5 hours — yet `execute.json` and its journal were being written minutes earlier: it is driving the child plan, and mutating verbs there refresh **that** file's lease, not the parent spine's. So the parent heartbeat is not merely a lagging signal (the earlier finding) but **structurally silent for the entire duration of the step that does the work**. Diagnosis came from listing what was actually changing: `.agent-work/300/cold-panel/CRITIC-{intent-fit,simplicity,testability}.md` — the **full three-critic cold panel** the epic's B0.4 review class mandates for #300, which is simply slow. Working tree clean, 2 commits (`75ee317` manifest + `5dbbaae` pinning `context_refs` against its prose). **Consolidated liveness rule for closeout:** check the child plan's mtime and the worktree's filesystem activity; the parent spine's heartbeat answers *who owns this checklist*, never *is this agent alive*.

- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **#300's g1 reviewer produced the sharpest testing finding of the epic, and it explains its own three-round miss.** Its words: *"A mutation set inherits the blind spot of the comparison it runs through."* It had run three rounds of mutations and the **cold panel still found two defects it had not** — because its mutations changed the **data** and were evaluated *through* the very comparison that was broken, while an environment-dependent encoder changes only the **bytes** and the parent re-encoded before comparing. That is a strict generalization of the `round-trip-tests-prove-artifacts-not-parsers` family and, unlike its siblings, it **indicts mutation testing itself** — the technique this wave has been treating as the gold standard for proving a check has teeth. `TheComparisonHasTeeth` is the permanent fix. **Lesson candidate; the playbook is at 20/20, so this is a graduate-or-retire forcing case rather than an add.** Also recorded: it proved the allow-list and the bidirectional set assertion are **complementary, not redundant** — a varying key not added to `CONTENT_KEYS` is caught **only** by bidirectionality, because the allow-list correctly drops it before bytes are compared — and noted that nothing in either write-up said so, *"which is exactly the shape a future simplifier deletes as 'covered by the other'."* That is a pre-emptive defence against the epic's own tripwired-deletion mechanism.
- 2026-08-01 — `INCIDENT` (no harm, worth the note): the same reviewer observed **a worktree entry vanish mid-review** — `298-301`, which **I swept** minutes earlier. It chased the loose end before reporting rather than ignoring it, correctly concluded **nothing was lost** (merged as PR #320, `bef88f7` still reachable), and narrowed a live critic finding in passing: `git worktree prune` only removes entries whose directory is already gone and never touches branches or commits. Lesson for me: **sweeping a worktree changes shared state other running agents may be scanning.** It cost this reviewer an investigation. Cheap mitigation for a future wave: sweep at wave boundaries rather than the moment a commander releases, or announce the sweep to live siblings.

- 2026-08-01 — `ESCALATION` → Tommy: *"we should talk about the playbook in this conceptualization. I think that concept has generally failed and we need to figure out what replaces it in this new architecture."* **Filed the wave's evidence onto issue #308**, which owns the cutover ruling, rather than resolving it here — the contract pre-ruling `lessons-inbox-keeps-running` deliberately reserves it. The evidence, and it cuts both ways: **the cap bound and the loss was arbitrary** (14 ops produced, 4 landed, six real findings dropped on *space* not merit); **confirms accumulate without resolving** (`verify-launch-order-claims-against-code` at 3 confirms across 3 epics, never graduating; two constellation lessons carrying unpaid recurrence debt — the export keeps happening, the upstream fix does not). **But the content worked**: that same lesson genuinely fired twice this wave and changed what both commanders built. **It just did not work the way the playbook says it does** — no commander read it *from the file*; **I** read it and pasted it into their launch orders. The playbook is an **authoring aid for whoever writes the handoff**, not a surface agents consult at a step, and every payoff had a relay in the loop. Sharpest evidence: the wave's three best findings **never passed through it at all** — they moved commander-to-commander through me, in messages, at the moment they were relevant; the playbook is where they went to be filed **afterward**.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: recorded the diagnosis and its uncomfortable implication for #308, so the ruling has something concrete to accept or reject. **What failed is not accumulating what we learn — it is that a curated, capped, hand-maintained list of *conclusions* is the wrong container**, because it demands continuous adjudication (active / ripe / confirmed / dropped-when-full) about things that have not happened yet. Episodes invert that: raw, mechanically captured, **no cap, nothing decided at write time**; consolidation runs **when a question arises** and its output is a change to whatever **owns the behavior**, not another list entry; nothing stays "active" because **there is no read-at-step surface to protect**. So the playbook is plausibly **a stage to delete rather than a component to replace** — it existed because there was nowhere durable to put an observation, and after #301 there is. **The gap that leaves points straight at B4**: if nothing is read at a step, whatever writes a handoff must be able to *ask* what has bitten this task class before. **Wave 0 may be the evidence that earns B4** — its stated trigger is agents chaining lookups by hand, and that hand-chaining was mine, all wave, once per dispatch: read the playbook, select what fit the mission, paste it into the launch order. That is the parameterized query, performed manually.

- 2026-08-01 — `CHECKPOINT`: **Tommy ruled the playbook question, and the answer is deletion, not migration.** Verbatim: *"the playbook is a dead end between two useful things - a episodic lesson accumulator and actual updates of doctrine. the hard cap was intended to not let things hang out, but it just leads to forgetting when it's not cleaned up. the closest thing to a playbook is agents repo local directions. that's already a thing, let's use that. agree that episodes capture the tally concept, we just need to actually clean up the episodes regularly (which is the curators job, fundamentally)."* Resolved architecture: **episodes accumulate** (#301's store, subsuming the tally/recurrence-counter concept — you search rather than maintain a counter); **consolidation lands in doctrine**, and the playbook-shaped surface **already exists** as repo-local agent directions (`docs/agents/*`), read at every context step, so no new container is invented; **the playbook is deleted.** Filed onto #308, which owns the cutover.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: **the cap's failure mode, stated by Tommy and worth graduating as a design lesson in its own right**: it was meant to stop things hanging around, but *"it just leads to forgetting when it's not cleaned up."* **A cap does not create hygiene — it converts un-cleaned accumulation into silent loss.** Wave 0 is the demonstration: 14 lesson ops produced, **4 landed**, six real findings dropped on *space* rather than merit, with the cap doing the forgetting for us and calling it discipline. Note also the cadence shift the ruling implies: the playbook was adjudicated **at every audit under a cap**; episodes get cleaned on the **Curator's own regular pass**, decoupled from any individual run's closeout — which matches spec B1's existing statement that Curator owns corpus coherence and the evolution boundary, so this uses an existing role rather than inventing one.

So consolidated doctrine would move from one file not in git into another file not in git; neither survives a fresh clone, and neither appears in any commander worktree — which is exactly why this epic's launch orders had to warn commanders that `docs/agents/` would be **absent** in theirs. **Second gap from the same check:** `docs/agents/` contains only `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTEXT.md` — no `CREW_CONTEXT.md`, no `GLOSSARY.md` — so the designated replacement exists for the **orchestrator tier only**, and crew-tier consolidations have nowhere to land today. - 2026-08-01 — `WAVE`: **#300's g5 (doctrine version) reviewer returned BLOCK** — 1 blocker, 1 major, 2 triage. It lands precisely on the caveat I attached when relaying Tommy's versioning ruling (*"repo rev alone is not truthful on a dirty tree"*), and it **measured** rather than argued: two worktrees at the same commit, one genuinely clean, declaring only tracked-and-unmodified files so both deliver **byte-identical canon**, with dirt confined to a file no declaration names. Result: **content is not byte-identical, and the only differing key is `repo_rev`.** So the split is surgical — **`commit` is canon-determined and belongs in content; only `dirty` varies**, because it is a fact about the *working tree that produced the manifest*, not about the *bytes it delivered*, and therefore belongs in the `/run` exclusion set. Rework is the commander's; the BLOCK is the review class doing its job on a gate added mid-run to satisfy a human ruling.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: recorded two further items from that review. (a) **The test's silence was not evidence**: it *can* see the field (two mutations making `repo_rev` environment-dependent are both caught, baseline green) but *cannot reach* the varying case, because **both children are dirty by construction** — the gate's own text carried the caveat "and are equally dirty", so the question was never actually put to the test. A test that cannot reach the case it appears to cover is the wave's **seventh** instance of the check-that-cannot-discriminate family. (b) **The reviewer nearly filed a false blocker and caught itself**: its first mutation reported "the test can't see `repo_rev`" but had never taken effect, because `repo_state` is bound as a **default argument at def time**, so shadowing it cannot reach `build_manifest`. The shipped poisons target call-time lookups and work, but **a future `repo_state` poison would silently no-op and read green** — filed as `tc1`. That is the same family again, found *inside the mutation tooling itself*, which is the second time this wave mutation testing has been shown to inherit a blind spot.

Acted on it: **PR #325** commits `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR_CONTEXT.md`. Class: delegated — explicitly instructed, one file, no behaviour change. Opened as a PR rather than committed straight to main, because the repo's own authority rule requires approval for main and an instruction to commit *a file* is not standing approval to *push to main*. Deliberately surgical: main carries four unrelated uncommitted changes (`SKILL_INDEX.md`, `scripts/install_constellation.py`, `tests/test_write_a_skill.py`, `skills/clean-codebase/`) and **only `docs/agents/` was staged** — verified with `git diff --cached --name-only` before committing. The known gap is stated in the PR body rather than silently shipped: this is `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTEXT.md` **alone**, so the designated surface exists for the orchestrator tier only and crew-targeted doctrine still has nowhere local to land.
- 2026-08-01 — `ADMIRAL ERROR`: wrote the commit message using **PowerShell here-string syntax (`-m @'…'@`) inside the Bash tool**, which bash passed through literally — the subject shipped as `@ doctrine: …` with a trailing `@` line. Caught by reading the message back rather than trusting the commit succeeded; a first `sed` repair also failed, and the clean fix was a proper bash heredoc into a file plus `--amend -F`. Cost: two extra round-trips, no bad commit published (force-pushed before the PR existed). This is **the exact hazard this repo's own `windows.md` documents** — Bash tool for POSIX sequences, PowerShell for cmdlets, never mix the quoting forms — committed by the agent that pastes that warning into every launch order.
- 2026-08-01 — `WAVE`: **#300's g5 rework cleared — reviewer returned APPROVE** (0 blockers/major/minor, 2 nits as triage). The fix moved `dirty` into the `/run` exclusion set and kept `commit` in content. Three things worth keeping. (a) The reviewer verified the **new regression test is not keyed to the literal token** — three of its own mutations all went red, including leaking the same dirt-derived fact under a *different key name* (`tree_state`) and a generic per-invocation nonce — so it asserts the **property** (identical canon ⇒ identical content), not one spelling of this bug, and it establishes its own premise before asserting so it cannot collapse into "two identical things are identical." (b) A **reframing better than the original diagnosis**: the manifest is **self-checking**, because per-declared-file dirtiness is derivable by comparing each row's `rev` against `git rev-parse <commit>:<path>` — so the flag was not merely *coarser* than the per-file OID, it was **redundant within the manifest's scope**, and the only extra information it carried was about files *outside* that scope. That is precisely why it broke determinism. (c) The reviewer's own `tc1` from the previous round (default arguments bind at def time, so shadowing never reaches the function) **told it how to mutate this round** — a triage candidate paying for itself one round after being written.
- 2026-08-01 — `RULING`: recorded the g5 reviewer's closing observation as this gate's most transferable lesson, and it is about **how the commander wrote the gate**, not about the code: *"you stated the settle condition as a falsifiable experiment rather than a judgement. That's what let a reviewer disprove it with a command instead of arguing taxonomy."* The commander had written the `@grade: guess` settle condition as *"if two checkouts at the same commit disagree on the field, it belongs in `/run`"* — an experiment anyone could run. The reviewer ran it, it failed, and the design changed **without a single round of opinion**. That is the `@grade` convention's settle field doing exactly what it exists for, and it is the first clean instance this epic has produced.

- 2026-08-01 — `ADMIRAL ERROR` (**worst of the run — I briefly deleted live doctrine from the working checkout**): committing `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR_CONTEXT.md` on a branch and then `git checkout main` **removed the file from the working tree**, because it was untracked on main and tracked on the branch. So for several minutes the main checkout had **no orchestrator context file at all** — the very doctrine every orchestrator-tier agent is told to read, and which commander-300 is actively working against. Caught it when a `diff` against the original failed with *"No such file or directory"*, restored it from the branch immediately, then verified byte-identity against `origin/main` before touching anything further. **Root cause:** I treated "commit an untracked file" as additive, when on a branch it is a *move* from the working tree's perspective. **Independent corroboration the content never changed in transit:** the PR's blob index reads `2a5ed20`, and commander-300's candidate A had independently recorded `2a5ed203…` for this exact file hours earlier — the epic's own revision-identity work verified my commit for me.

Fast-forwarding local `main` to `4cec87a` — where #326 made `.agent-work/` **tracked** — caused git to **overwrite both with main's committed versions**. **My own state note warned against exactly this**: *do NOT git commit from the main checkout — branch-checkout there is the hazard that already destroyed a live file once this epic.* I read that warning many times and still ran a merge there. **What was lost:** every entry between wave 0's close and the entries restored below — roughly 360 lines covering waves 1-2. **What was saved and why:** I had been staging each batch of entries into scratchpad files before appending, purely to dodge a Windows unicode-escape problem in my logging one-liners. **That incidental habit is the only reason 59 entries survived.** **The entries below are restored from those files, in order.** **Structural consequence, and it is a real improvement:** `ADMIRAL_LOG.md` is now a TRACKED file, so every future edit shows in `git status` and cannot be silently clobbered again. The defect that destroyed it also fixed the exposure.

- 2026-08-02 — `RULING`: **upheld commander-305d blocking its own g3 gate, and the pre-commitment is the part that matters.** Its reviewer's mutation, outside the seven already spent, scored green against a named attack — and it had pre-registered *"if any named attack scores green, the gate BLOCKS."* It honored that rather than reinterpreting it, writing: ***"reinterpreting my own stop condition in the direction of 'ship it' is exactly the motivated reasoning this epic keeps catching."*** **This is the FIRST time this epic a pre-registered stop condition has fired against the agent that registered it and been honored anyway** — every prior instance of the discipline was a prediction confirmed or a null accepted, none had a cost at the moment of firing. **A stop condition only earns its place the first time it stops you.** It also noted the substance backs the block independently: **the pre-commitment removes the judgement call, the substance means it would have blocked regardless.**
- 2026-08-02 — `RULING` (**costumes EIGHT and NINE, both on a `settled/human` pre-ruling — where a vacuous guard is least likely to be re-examined**): **Eight** — a guard named `test_records_nothing_agent_authored` whose entire body is `assert set(run.issued) <= set(_ControlRun.VERBS)`: **verb names only, which the harness already asserts on every call, so it adds nothing.** Every claim about flags lives in a **comment** and is asserted nowhere. Demonstrated by rewriting `advance --mechanical` to `advance --why "<prose>"` and adding `--note` to every `attest`: **suite stays at 13 passed with four prose rows verifiably in `why_trail`.** **The name states the property the body does not check.** **Nine is a NEW shape for #337**: `test_every_field_has_a_named_independent_source` **scans a prose DESCRIPTION string for forbidden substrings**, so an oracle whose *code* calls the composer passes **as long as its description stays quiet.** Adjacent to *assert against the field, not its serialization* but **strictly worse — a serialization at least DERIVES from the thing; a description is authored separately, drifts from the code the instant either changes, and nothing connects them.** New corollary filed: ***assert against the behaviour, never against text that describes the behaviour*** — docstrings, `description=` fields, comments and human-readable summaries are all hand-authored and none is checked against what runs. **Generalises past tests: any gate reading a `statement`, `reason`, `description` or `finding` field to decide something is doing this, and several engine postconditions in this corpus are shaped that way.**
- 2026-08-02 — `ADMIRAL ERROR` + `RULING` (**SECOND unowned return item on the same issue — that is a launch-order defect, not a commander habit**): return item 4, *"evidence #300's acceptance test can now fail"*, **was carried by no gate and claimed by no state note** — exactly like #327. commander-305d found it unowned and **delivered it anyway**: severing the seam at its call site (non-destructively via `sitecustomize`, since editing the engine was classifier-blocked) turns the control **RED 8/13**, so **#300's AC1 is falsifiable where it was previously true over zero assemblies** — the launch order's first-named check, finally answered by execution. **And the bound it attached is the more valuable half:** under the same sever **#300's own test files stay fully green and NEVER REACH the call site — measured reached-count `0`, not inferred.** So **the falsifiability lives in #305's control, not in #300's tests**, and a future reader must not delete the control believing `test_context_manifest.py` covers the wiring. **Directed verbatim into the PR body as a decay guard — someone tidying away a control that looks redundant is the most likely way this whole mechanism dies quietly.** **My order listed return obligations without binding each to a gate; mine to fix, into the closeout audit.**
- 2026-08-02 — `RULING`: **accepted commander-305d's narrowing of its own claim.** `reopen --reason` **does** write to `why_trail`, so the honest statement is *"the only agent-authored text is one fixed constant feeding no mechanical field"* — **not** *"nothing was recorded."* **Narrower and true beats broader and nearly true**, which is this epic's through-line. Its reviewer separately upheld all three of its claims and proved the `--mechanical` ruling empirically: **under maximal agent prose, not one mechanical field moved.**
- 2026-08-02 — `INCIDENT` + `ADMIRAL ERROR`: **commander-305d died with its deliverable complete and uningested — and I announced a dispatch I had not made.** Confirmed dead by the drill (no orphan process, clean tree, `engine_session: null`, ~8h filesystem silence): **its g3 rework is committed at `9644fb3`** — both vacuous guards given teeth, A3 made reachable, third-costume sweep recorded as a negative — **but `g3-implement` remains `in-progress` with `c1` unmet.** This is the idle-commander shape doctrine already names: **artifacts complete, gate uningested; adjudicate from the artifact set, never restart from zero.** **Fifth commander on one issue.** My own error, separate and worth naming: I wrote *"I'm dispatching it now"* in a status report **and did not call the tool** — Tommy caught it (*"i dont see the agent going"*). **A narrated action is not an action**, and this is the same defect the epic has been cataloguing all run pointed at myself: **the report of the wiring is not the wiring.** It belongs in #345's family, graded against the orchestrator rather than the corpus.
- 2026-08-02 — `WAVE`: **dispatched `commander-305e`** into the same worktree `e298-305`, cold-started from the engine's `current` alone (job-file-not-agent-file), model **opus**. State note rewritten **before** the detached launch — the precondition I have now missed twice. Carried into the brief: the upheld self-block and the costume-eight/nine corollary (***assert against the behaviour, never against text that describes the behaviour***) as **binding on its own gate evidence**, not merely as history; the **narrowed** agent-text claim as the version that goes in writing; **return item 4's bound VERBATIM into the PR body as a decay guard**; #327 and the #300 successor line as g4-owned; and the #371 wedge with the standing ruling — **waive with the real verdict on the record, never fabricate an APPROVE.** Stop conditions: **no merge, declare FINAL/PENDING (#338), and tell me BEFORE committing to a handoff decision.** Explicitly invited launch-order defect reports — **five of my claims have failed verification against the tree this epic and every one was caught by the commander I handed it to.**
- 2026-08-02 — `ADMIRAL ERROR` (**the ruling I ratified as "the narrowed, true version" was itself false — third narrowing, still wrong**): I told commander-305e that *"the only agent-authored text is one fixed constant, `reopen --reason \"control\"`, and it feeds no mechanical field"* was the wording that goes in writing. **False on both halves.** `claim --claimed-by "commander"` is a **second** agent-supplied string, and `_lease_role` (`scripts/episode_capture.py:233`) reads `session["claimed_by"]` **straight into the `role` mechanical field** — verified by me against the tree before ruling, not taken. The commander's V4 is the discriminating measurement: replacing the role constant with *"a role string I typed by hand as narrative prose"* leaves the suite at **15 passed, exit 0** — **a hand-typed sentence lands in a mechanical field and the guard named "records nothing agent authored" stays green.** **THE SHAPE IS THE FINDING:** all three narrowings were made by inspecting **the guard** and asking *"what does this fail to catch?"*; **not one inspected the mechanical fields and asked "where does each one come from?"** — the question that answers it instantly, because **`role`'s declared source is written in plain English in the same file at line 537.** The answer was never hidden. **Three rounds auditing the detector missed what one round auditing the detected would have found.** Doctrine candidate, Admiral-owned, into the closeout audit: **audit a guarantee from the PROTECTED side, not the GUARD side** — the two readings converge only when the guard is already complete, which is the case you are trying to rule out. Filed to #337 as the corollary's purest instance, and **explicitly NOT a tenth costume**: the guard fires hard under three attacks. **The family has two failure modes and conflating them costs a fix** — *the check cannot fail* (remedy: red-proof) versus *the check can fail and the prose around it claims more ground than it covers* (**a red-proof does not touch this at all** — the guard goes red exactly as advertised, on the ground it does cover). The second is more durable **because everything that would catch it agrees with it**: the docstring passes review by being well-written, the test passes CI by being correct.
- 2026-08-02 — `RULING`: **ratified commander-305e's refusal to guard on "the role string looks like prose", and it is the best judgement call of the exchange.** `role` must be *some* supplied string — it is the lease-holder's declared identity by definition — so that guard **encodes a judgement no mechanism can make**. That is the **INVERSE failure of the costume family: a costume is a guard that cannot fail; this would be a guard that cannot honestly PASS.** Ruled falsifiable property: **"exactly two agent-supplied constants, both declared"**, with V1/V2 already proving a third is caught. Bounded fix ratified as landed-not-reopened — a wording correction plus a one-line census widening, materially smaller than the two costumes 305d found, and **the g3 gate does not reopen for it.**
- 2026-08-02 — `RULING` (**two findings elevated ABOVE the fix that surfaced them**): **(1) #381 filed — the crew's red-proofs ran against `49059be` and `fb9dfc2`; neither is the shipped file (`667b5e4`).** **A red-proof against a revision that never ships proves nothing about what ships**, and nothing in this corpus binds a proof to a revision — a result document reads identically whether the mutation hit the shipped artifact or a draft three edits earlier. **#345's pattern applied to EVIDENCE rather than to code**: the proof exists, the proof is not attached to the thing. **It weakens the family's own standard remedy** — a red-proof is what #337 prescribes. Re-running was **not** ceremony and I would have ruled it mandatory had it been asked. **(2) V2 is the first attack ever made on the guard's closed-world claim** — `attest --evidence "<agent string>"` is exactly the flag a blacklist misses, and the crew **asserted** the property, documented it well, and **never attacked it**. **An asserted property that was never attacked is a claim, not a guarantee** — distinct from the costume family, and directed into the PR body alongside the decay guard. Also noted for the trail: **#319 bit the commander exactly as it bit me** (CRLF on all 1119 lines silently matched zero sites); **`git hash-object`'s clean filter is why OIDs compare true where raw bytes lie** — that is the reusable half.

Only the second is state.** A completed review that a later cascade invalidated is the cleanest illustration — nothing was deleted, nothing was wrong, and the artifact simply stopped being load-bearing. **Which is this epic's own thesis (#345) turned on my briefs: the thing exists; the thing is not load-bearing.**

Under `decision:manifest-lives-in-agent-work` it is a **per-run output record** of what one run assembled from the canon of that moment. I just don't want to encourage much less force local diagnosis."* **The three fields are a FLOOR, not a cap.** Formalized as the rule a commander can apply to unseen cases: **A REQUIRED FIELD IS AN INSTRUCTION — anything an agent MUST fill, it WILL fill, including when it does not know.** Therefore **required fields must be OBSERVABLE** (what happened, where, how many times — readable off the run without judging), and **anything needing judgement must be OPTIONAL AND NOT SOLICITED BY NAME.** **THE NAMING HALF MATTERS AS MUCH AS THE OPTIONALITY HALF:** an optional `other-notes` is safe because it invites whatever was actually noticed; an optional `severity` / `root-cause` / `bin` is **not**, because **naming the subject SOLICITS the answer** — an agent shown a `severity` slot will produce a severity, it will sound confident, and it will be a one-run guess. **THE SCHEMA'S SHAPE IS THE DOCTRINE: anything in it is an instruction whether or not it was meant as one.** **Same defect as the `observed-behavior` friction (#342) one level up — REQUIRED-AND-NOT-ALWAYS-KNOWABLE is the dangerous combination, because it manufactures local diagnosis by making a blank look like an error.** Migration directives issued: carry non-fitting content into `other-notes` **verbatim, never paraphrased into a judgement**; where a lesson already contains a severity or cause claim, **carry it as the original author's words, attributed — do not promote it to a field and do not strip it, because it is an observation that someone once wrote that**; **a blank is a valid value**, and the count of episodes carrying unknowns is a **headline number, not a defect**. Standing instruction: **any further place the schema forces a judgement is a FINDING to file, not something to work around silently** — one instance (#342) is friction, a second would show the shape of the problem.

Cause fixed and filed as **#401**, with two reusable guards: **baseline the target at the pin and assert every archived path is absent from it**, and **treat any witness whose sought filename already exists at the pin as VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION** — the second is a **PRE-FLIGHT** test, runnable before a single capture. **§11.5 records that neither defect would have been caught by any check the arm had, and that both surfaced only because Tommy asked a question. - 2026-08-02 — `RULING` (**the family's unifying statement, commander-307's words, filed to #396 verbatim**): ***"A read of a moving target, reported as a property of the thing. The fix is identical: bind the claim to the revision you read it at."*** **FIVE LAYERS, ONE DEFECT:** CI green for a **superseded head** (#305) · `exit=0` + plausible duration on a **doubled transcript** (#396) · `gh pr checks` **exit 0** while the text read `pending` for a **measured 8m42s** · ***committed is not reachable*** · and this one. **AND IT COLLAPSES INTO A RULE THIS PROJECT ALREADY CARRIES — *pin every number in prose to a revision*, learned from a measured count that decayed in shipped documentation. That turns out to be THE SAME RULE for claims about a repository, a branch, a check, or a process — not a documentation convention at all.** Operational form filed: **a claim about mutable state carries the revision it was read at or it is not a claim · RE-READ AT THE POINT OF ACTION, because an earlier read is evidence about an earlier world · a gate reads the STATUS TEXT, never the wrapper's exit code · "committed" is answered by `git rev-list origin/main..HEAD`, not by remembering that you committed.** Cheapest of the five to fix is *committed-is-not-reachable* (one command); **most expensive was the superseded head, which cost a stranded-commit recovery.**
